You are producing an independent transcription of the IRS worksheet or form below into a
formal data model, working ONLY from the IRS text provided here. Do not rely on memory of the tax law
or of any software; if the text does not say it, do not encode it.

# Data model (from the project specification)

### 4.1 Values and types

| Type | Representation |
|---|---|
| `Dollars` | Integer cents, signed |
| `NonNegDollars` | `Dollars` known to be zero or more |
| `Rate` | Pair of integers, numerator and denominator |
| `Count` | Non-negative integer |
| `Bool` | True or false |
| `Enum` | A named finite set, such as filing status or Form 8949 box |
| `Date` | Calendar date, for holding period only. Reserved: not in `schema/data.schema.json` and used by no line; a sale's holding period is answered as its short- or long-term box (`sales.box`) |

Every value also has a completeness state: unknown, placeholder, or known. A return can be produced only when every reachable line is known.

### 4.2 Atoms

| Atom | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `input` | A value the taxpayer supplies, tied to a source document box or an interview question |
| `copy` | The value of another line, on this or another form |
| `param` | A year-pinned constant, optionally keyed by filing status |
| `sum` | Add a list of lines: all money, or all `Count` lines (a `Count`) |
| `subtract` | Signed subtraction |
| `subtract_floor0` | Subtract; if zero or less, zero |
| `smaller_of`, `larger_of` | Minimum or maximum of two lines |
| `multiply_rate` | Amount times a rate, half-up to the cent, or rounded once to a stated unit |
| `multiply_count` | Amount times a whole number (a `Count` line) |
| `prorate` | Amount times (part ÷ whole), the ratio rounded half-up to stated decimal places and kept between 0 and 1 |
| `percent_floor` | A whole-number percentage of part over whole, decimals dropped, with a cap (cap + 1 above it) |
| `round` | Round to a stated unit by a stated rule |
| `table_lookup` | Banded lookup in a pinned table |
| `keyed_lookup` | The entry of a pinned table (a rate, an amount, a count or an enum value) for a tuple of enum or count keys |
| `rate_lookup` | The rate of a pinned table of "over / but not over" bands of a money key, optionally by filing status |
| `bracket_schedule` | Tax from a rate schedule |
| `sum_over` | Total of one column over a collection, with an optional filter |
| `test` | A comparison yielding `Bool`, with `all`, `any`, `not` |
| `choose` | Guarded alternatives; the guards must be exhaustive and disjoint |
| `opaque` | A named host-language function with its own citation and tests |

There are no user-defined functions, loops, local variables, strings or recursion. Adding an atom requires a written justification, a rendering template, a Lean definition and its lemmas. The vocabulary is cross-checked against the IRS Fact Graph operator list before it is frozen.

**Additions after the freeze.** `multiply_count` (Stage 10). Justification: the Simplified Method Worksheet (Form 1040 instructions, lines 5a and 5b, line 5) says "Multiply line 4 by the number of months for which this year's payments were made"; the multiplier is a whole number the filer supplies, which `multiply_rate` cannot express because its rate is a year parameter. The atom multiplies cents by a non-negative integer, exactly, with no rounding. Rendering template: "Multiply ⟨amount⟩ by the number on ⟨count⟩." Lean definition `multiplyCount` (`lean/TaxFormalization/Atoms.lean`) with lemmas for non-negativity, monotonicity in each argument, a zero count and repeated addition (`multiplyCount_succ`), in `Lemmas.lean`. The owner approved the addition over restricting the worksheet to twelve-month years. `prorate` (Stage 11). Justification: the Student Loan Interest Deduction Worksheet (Form 1040 instructions, Schedule 1, line 21) says at line 7 "Divide line 6 by $15,000 ($30,000 if married filing jointly). Enter the result as a decimal (rounded to at least three places). If the result is 1.000 or more, enter 1.000" and at line 8 "Multiply line 1 by line 7"; dividing one amount by another is not expressible with `multiply_rate`, whose rate is a year parameter. The atom takes an amount, a part (a money line), a whole (a money operand) and a number of places; the ratio is rounded half-up to that many places (three, the reading recorded as `amb.2025.sli_ratio_places`) and kept between 0 and 1, and the product is rounded half-up to the cent as `multiply_rate` is. Rendering template: "Divide ⟨part⟩ by ⟨whole⟩, rounded to ⟨places⟩ decimal places; if the result is 1 or more, use 1 (and 0 if it is less than 0). Multiply ⟨amount⟩ by the result." Lean definition `prorate` (`lean/TaxFormalization/Atoms.lean`) with lemmas for non-negativity, never exceeding the amount, monotonicity in the amount and the part, and antitonicity in the whole, in `Lemmas.lean`. The owner approved the addition over refusing the phase-out range. `keyed_lookup` (Stage 14). Justification: Form 4562 and Pub. 946 compute depreciation from printed tables keyed by facts that are not income: the MACRS percentage tables (i4562 column (g), "Multiply column (c) by the applicable rate from the appropriate table"; Pub. 946 Appendix A) by recovery period, convention or quarter and year of recovery; the limits for passenger automobiles (i4562 Tables 1 to 4) by date placed in service and number of tax years in service; and the class lives and recovery periods (Pub. 946 Appendix B) by asset class. `table_lookup` bands a money key and returns money, and `multiply_rate` takes its rate from a year parameter, so none of these can be expressed without spelling each table out as hundreds of guarded parameters. The atom returns the entry of the row whose keys equal the key lines' values, or unknown when no row has them; the checker requires distinct key tuples, keys and entries of the declared types, and rates between 0 and 1. Rendering template: "Find the entry in ⟨table⟩ for ⟨key⟩ ⟨line⟩ and ...". Lean definition `keyedLookup` (`lean/TaxFormalization/Atoms.lean`) with lemmas that a found entry is a row of the table with those keys, that there is none exactly when no row has them, that with distinct keys every row's entry is found, and that a table of rates between 0 and 1 yields one (`Lemmas.lean`). `multiply_rate` accepts a Rate line as its rate. The owner approved the addition over spelling the tables out and over narrowing the depreciation scope. `rate_lookup` (Stage 15). Justification: Form 8880 line 9 says "Enter the applicable decimal amount from the table below", a table of "Over... But not over..." bands of line 8 by filing status, and Form 2441 line 8 says "Enter on line 8 the decimal amount shown below that applies to the amount on line 7", a table of "Over / But not over" bands with one column; both results are rates. `table_lookup` returns money from bands of the form [at least, less than), `keyed_lookup` keys only on enum or count values, and a `choose` returns money, so the rates could only be reached by spelling each table out as guarded parameters and one product per band. The atom's table stores the bands as printed: each band has "over" and "but not over" bounds and one rate per column (filing-status columns as for `table_lookup`, or one column). The checker requires the bands to be contiguous with increasing bounds, the first open below and the last open above, every filing status in exactly one column, every rate between 0 and 1, and the line declared `Rate`. The atom returns the rate of the first band whose upper bound is at least the key, which with those bands is the one with over < key ≤ but not over. Rendering template: "Find the rate in ⟨table⟩ for the band (over, but not over) that holds ⟨key⟩, in the column for the filing status (⟨status⟩)." Lean definition `rateBand` (`lean/TaxFormalization/Atoms.lean`) with lemmas that a found rate is that of a band holding the key, that every earlier band ends below the key, that a table with a band open above gives every key a rate, that a table of rates between 0 and 1 yields one, and that the band reached is monotone in the key (`Lemmas.lean`). Mutation testing shifts each band's upper bound by $1 and each band's rates by one point. The owner chose the atom over letting a `choose` return a rate and over spelling the tables out.

**Additions for Stage 18 (owner's decision D2, 2026-10-28).** Three, each needed by Form 8962 and nothing else
expressing it. (a) `percent_floor` (part, whole, cap): Form 8962 Worksheet 2 (i8962 p. 9) says "Enter 401 here and on
line 5 of Form 8962" when household income is more than four times the poverty line, and otherwise "Divide the amount
on line 1 above by the amount on line 2 above. Do not round; instead, multiply this number by 100 (to express it as a
percentage) and then drop any numbers after the decimal point"; no atom divides money by money to a whole number. The
result, a `Count`, is cap + 1 when 100 × part > cap × whole, otherwise floor(100 × part ÷ whole), not below zero; the
whole must be positive (the line is unknown otherwise). Rendering template: "If ⟨part⟩ is more than ⟨cap⟩% of ⟨whole⟩,
enter ⟨cap + 1⟩. Otherwise divide ⟨part⟩ by ⟨whole⟩, multiply by 100 and drop any numbers after the decimal point."
Lean definition `percentFloor` with lemmas that it is not negative, at most cap + 1, the largest whole number p with
p × whole ≤ 100 × part below the cap, and monotone in the part. (b) `sum` over `Count` lines: Form 8962 line 1, tax
family size, adds the people on the return and the dependents claimed, two counts; mixing money and counts is a checker
error. (c) `multiply_rate` with an optional `unit` (positive whole cents): Form 8962 line 8a, "Multiply line 3 by line
7. Round to nearest whole dollar amount", is one rounding of the exact product; rounding to the cent and then to the
dollar differs on about 0.5% of household incomes (the premise map's verification, E2). With `unit` the exact product
is rounded half-up once to a multiple of the unit. Rendering template: "Multiply ⟨amount⟩ by ⟨rate⟩, rounded to the
nearest multiple of ⟨unit⟩ (half up)." Lean definition `multiplyRateToUnit` with lemmas that the result is a multiple
of the unit and is monotone in the amount.

### 4.3 Lines

Each line of a form or worksheet is one record:

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| `id` | Stable identifier that survives renumbering between years |
| `printed` | The line number or letter as printed in this revision |
| `label` | The IRS wording for the line |
| `type` | One of the types in 4.1 |
| `expr` | One atom application |
| `cite` | Citation (section 5) |
| `ambiguity` | Optional reference into the ambiguity register |
| `display` | Optional: why a line no other line reads is kept (it is shown on the values sheet) |
| `shown_when` | Optional, on a printed line: a `Bool` line of the same document; the values sheet shows the line only when it is true, for a per-entry line the form reports for some entries only (a Form 4562 asset's line 14 only for 2025 property that is not listed). The value is computed either way. |

A form definition is a list of lines in printed order, plus the form's identity, tax year and revision.



Expression grammar (YAML). Exactly one atom per line. Operands written as a line reference
(a string id of a line in this worksheet or another form), a constant ({const: 0} for money cents,
{const: n, type: Count} for a nonnegative whole-number count, {const: true}, {const: "single"}),
or a parameter ({param: name}). An untyped numeric constant is money cents, never a Count.
When comparing a Count line with a numeric threshold, mark the constant with type: Count.
Constants belong only in operand positions. A copy source or choose branch must be a line id
or a definition instance, not a constant/parameter object. Use op: param to define a constant helper
from the supplied parameter catalog. choose returns money; it does not select Count, Bool or Enum values.
Every test position (all/any entries, not, a choose guard or sum_over.where) requires a nested
test object. A bare Bool line id is an operand, not a test: wrap it as
{op: test, eq: [<Bool line id>, {const: true}]} (or compare with false).
sum adds money lines only; use sum_over for Count entry fields. A sum_over at return level may
read a root collection. Within a collection entry it may read only that entry's child collection;
it cannot sum another root collection. Use a published return-level aggregate where the IRS calls
for that aggregate. NonNegDollars declarations must be proved nonnegative by the expression types;
copying a Dollars input does not prove that property. Do not silently change a required result type.
subtract accepts money and always produces Dollars; it cannot subtract Counts. To work with
month counts, multiply the money amount by each count separately and subtract those money lines.
larger_of proves NonNegDollars only when every operand is already nonnegative in this checker;
use subtract_floor0 against zero when an IRS zero floor must establish a nonnegative result.

  {op: input, source: "<the box or question this value comes from>"}
  {op: copy, from: <ref>}                      # <ref> is a line id string, or for a parameterised
                                               # worksheet {def: tax_on_amount, bind: {amount: <ref>, status: <ref>}, line: tax}
  {op: param, ref: <parameter name>}
  {op: sum, of: [<ref>, ...]}
  {op: subtract, from: <ref>, take: <operand>}
  {op: subtract_floor0, from: <ref>, take: <operand>}     # if zero or less, enter -0-
  {op: smaller_of, of: [<ref>, <ref>]}
  {op: larger_of, of: [<ref>, <ref>]}
  {op: multiply_rate, amount: <ref>, rate: {param: name}, unit: <integer cents>}  # rate is a parameter; unit optional: the exact product rounded once, half-up, to that unit (100 = to the dollar); without it, half-up to the cent
  {op: multiply_count, amount: <ref>, count: <ref>}         # an amount times a whole number (a Count line), e.g. a monthly amount times months
  {op: prorate, amount: <ref>, part: <ref>, whole: <operand>, places: <int>}  # amount times (part / whole), the ratio rounded half-up to <places> decimal places and kept between 0 and 1
  {op: percent_floor, part: <ref>, whole: <operand>, cap: <int>}  # a whole-number percentage: cap + 1 when part is more than cap% of whole, else 100 x part / whole with the decimals dropped (not below 0)
  {op: keyed_lookup, table: <table id>, keys: [<ref>, ...]}  # the entry of a printed table (a rate, an amount, a number or a category) found by the values of the key lines, in the table's key order
  {op: rate_lookup, table: <table id>, key: <ref>, status: <ref>}  # the rate of a printed "Over / But not over" table for the amount on the key line; status (the filing status line) only when the table has filing-status columns
  {op: round, of: <ref>, unit: <integer cents>, rule: half_up | up | down}  # e.g. up to the next $1,000: unit 100000, rule up
  {op: table_lookup, table: tax_table, key: <ref>, status: <ref>}
  {op: sum_over, collection: <collection id>, field: <line id within the entry>, where: <test>}  # where optional
  {op: test, eq: [<operand>, <operand>]}        # also ne, lt, le, gt, ge; or {op: test, all: [<test>, ...]},
                                               # {op: test, any: [...]}, {op: test, not: <test>}
  {op: choose, alternatives: [{guard: <test>, then: <ref>}, ...]}   # guards exhaustive and disjoint

A line may also carry `refuses: [<id>, ...]`, a list of Bool inputs or lines: when any of them is true
the tool stops at this line and names the form the filer needs instead of computing it. The tool
supports only part of what some lines cover; the screening inputs listed below stand for the
situations it does not compute. Where the IRS text of a line covers such a situation, encode only the
case the tool computes and list the screening input under `refuses`, instead of adding inputs for the
unsupported amounts or writing the unsupported case as a zero.

For a consistency check on supplied figures, a refusal entry may instead be
{when: <Bool line id>, when_known: true}. A known true check still refuses, a refused gate
still propagates its refusal, and a known false check permits the expression. An unknown
consistency check is recorded as unperformed and does not block an independently determined
amount or request its missing figures. The expression must still determine its value;
this never fills an absent input with zero or false. Use ordinary refusal ids for eligibility
and scope questions that must be answered. Infer any consistency check from the source;
the project supplies no placement or formula.

Money is integer cents. Rates are parameters. There are no loops, strings, functions or arithmetic
beyond these atoms; a line that needs more than one atom is written as several lines, the extra ones
being helper lines (printed: "(helper)") with ids of your own choosing.

Exact JSON Schema for one expression (YAML must parse to this structure):
{"$schema":"https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema","$defs":{"expr":{"oneOf":[{"type":"object","required":["op","source"],"additionalProperties":false,"properties":{"op":{"const":"input"},"source":{"type":"string","minLength":1}}},{"type":"object","required":["op","from"],"additionalProperties":false,"properties":{"op":{"const":"copy"},"from":{"$ref":"#/$defs/lineRef"}}},{"type":"object","required":["op","ref"],"additionalProperties":false,"properties":{"op":{"const":"param"},"ref":{"type":"string","minLength":1}}},{"type":"object","required":["op","of"],"additionalProperties":false,"properties":{"op":{"const":"sum"},"of":{"type":"array","minItems":2,"items":{"type":"string"}}}},{"type":"object","required":["op","from","take"],"additionalProperties":false,"properties":{"op":{"const":"subtract"},"from":{"type":"string"},"take":{"$ref":"#/$defs/operand"}}},{"type":"object","required":["op","from","take"],"additionalProperties":false,"properties":{"op":{"const":"subtract_floor0"},"from":{"type":"string"},"take":{"$ref":"#/$defs/operand"}}},{"type":"object","required":["op","of"],"additionalProperties":false,"properties":{"op":{"const":"smaller_of"},"of":{"type":"array","minItems":2,"maxItems":2,"items":{"type":"string"}}}},{"type":"object","required":["op","of"],"additionalProperties":false,"properties":{"op":{"const":"larger_of"},"of":{"type":"array","minItems":2,"maxItems":2,"items":{"type":"string"}}}},{"type":"object","required":["op","amount","rate"],"additionalProperties":false,"properties":{"op":{"const":"multiply_rate"},"amount":{"type":"string"},"rate":{"$ref":"#/$defs/operand"},"unit":{"type":"integer","minimum":1}}},{"type":"object","required":["op","table","key","status"],"additionalProperties":false,"properties":{"op":{"const":"table_lookup"},"table":{"type":"string"},"key":{"type":"string"},"status":{"type":"string"}}},{"type":"object","required":["op","of","unit","rule"],"additionalProperties":false,"properties":{"op":{"const":"round"},"of":{"type" # The worksheet to transcribe

Form id: dependent
Title: Dependents — qualifying child and qualifying relative tests, per person listed
Tax year: 2025

Transcribe these printed lines in this order, using each exact id shown (shared definitions
may use bare ids such as line13; do not add a form prefix to those ids):
  - printed '2(d)' (id dependent.dc_line2d; type NonNegDollars)
  - printed '2(c)' (id dependent.dc_line2c; type Bool)
  - printed '27' (id dependent.edu_line27; type NonNegDollars)
  - printed '28' (id dependent.edu_line28; type NonNegDollars)
  - printed '29' (id dependent.edu_line29; type NonNegDollars)
  - printed '30' (id dependent.edu_line30; type NonNegDollars)
  - printed '31' (id dependent.edu_line31; type NonNegDollars)

The following result interfaces are required by other forms. If one is already a printed line
listed above, supply it once with that exact id and its printed marker. Otherwise supply a helper
with this exact id (printed "(helper)"). These names are not instructions for how to compute them.
Infer their meaning from the IRS pages; if the pages do not determine it, say so in a note rather
than guessing. Project-written labels and expressions are deliberately omitted:
  - id dependent.one (Count)
  - id dependent.claimed (Bool)
  - id dependent.ctc_child (Bool)
  - id dependent.odc_person (Bool)
  - id dependent.ctc_amount (NonNegDollars)
  - id dependent.odc_amount (NonNegDollars)
  - id dependent.actc_amount (NonNegDollars)
  - id dependent.hoh_absence (Bool)
  - id dependent.hoh_test2 (Bool)
  - id dependent.hoh_test1 (Bool)
  - id dependent.eic_child (Bool)
  - id dependent.eic_ssn_child (Bool)
  - id dependent.dc_qp (Bool)
  - id dependent.dc_count (Count)
  - id dependent.ptc_magi_counted (Dollars)

These input lines already exist and must be referenced by these exact ids (do not redefine them):
  - dependent.relationship (printed '(helper)', type Enum(dependent_relationship)): Relationship to you:

      - child (son, daughter, stepchild, eligible foster child, or a descendant of any of them)
        - an adopted child, including a child lawfully placed with you for legal adoption, is your son or daughter
        - an eligible foster child is one placed with you by an authorized placement agency or by judgment, decree, or other order of any court of competent jurisdiction
        - a child in your care without such a placement who is not otherwise your relative is entered as household_member or other
      - sibling (brother, sister, half brother, half sister, stepbrother or stepsister, or a son or daughter of a brother, sister, half brother or half sister)
      - sibling_descendant (any other descendant of a sibling, such as a grandniece or a stepsibling's child)
      - parent (father or mother; not a foster parent, who is entered as other)
      - parent_relative (a grandparent, aunt or uncle: an ancestor or sibling of a parent)
      - in_law (step-parent or in-law, including one whose relationship began with a marriage that has since ended by death or divorce)
      - household_member (any other person, other than a person who was your spouse at any time during 2025, who lived with you all year as a member of your household, if your relationship did not violate local law)
      - other (any other relationship; such a person is a Step 4 relative only if they lived with you all year as a member of your household and the relationship did not violate local law)

      Do not list a person who was your spouse at any time during 2025: that person can't be your qualifying relative.

      Give the relationship to you; on a joint return, a relative of your spouse who is no relative of yours is entered as household_member or other, and a later question asks about the relationship to your spouse.
  - dependent.age (printed '(helper)', type Count): Age at the end of 2025 (whole years)
  - dependent.student (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Was this person a full-time student in 2025, that is, during any part of 5 calendar months of 2025 (not necessarily consecutive), either of these?

      - enrolled for the hours or courses the school considers full-time attendance at a school that has a regular teaching staff and course of study and a regularly enrolled student body (an elementary school, junior or senior high school, college, university, or technical, trade or mechanical school)
      - taking a full-time, on-farm training course given by such a school or a state, county or local government agency

      A student who works on a "co-op" job in private industry as part of a school's regular course of classroom and practical training is a full-time student.

      An on-the-job training course, a correspondence school or a school offering courses only through the Internet does not count.
  - dependent.disabled (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Was this person permanently and totally disabled at any time in 2025, that is, unable to engage in any substantial gainful activity because of a physical or mental condition that a doctor has determined has lasted or can be expected to last continuously for at least a year or can be expected to lead to death?
  - dependent.months (printed '(helper)', type Count): Months this person lived with you in 2025 (0–12)

      - counting temporary absences for school, vacation, business, medical care, military service or detention in a juvenile facility as time lived with you
      - counting a newborn's required hospital stay following birth as time lived with you
      - but not counting time a child presumed kidnapped was missing because of the kidnapping

      Enter 7 if the person lived with you for more than half of 2025 but fewer than 7 full months.

      For a person born, died, adopted or placed with you in 2025 who meets the other conditions to be your qualifying child, enter 12 if your home was their home for more than half the time they were alive or with you, and 0 otherwise.

      For any other person born or who died in 2025, or adopted, lawfully placed with you for legal adoption, or placed with you as an eligible foster child in 2025, enter 12 only if your home was their home for the entire time they were alive or since the adoption or placement, and 0 otherwise
  - dependent.self_support (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Did this person provide over half of their own support for 2025?

      Total support is the amount spent for their food, lodging, clothing, education, medical and dental care, recreation, transportation and similar necessities, including tax-exempt income, savings and borrowed amounts used for their support and GI Bill tuition payments and allowances.

      It does not include:
      - income, social security or Medicare taxes paid from their own income
      - life insurance premiums
      - funeral expenses
      - medical insurance benefits (including Medicare benefits)
      - Survivors' and Dependents' Educational Assistance payments used for the support of the child who receives them

      Count the person's own funds only if actually spent for their support (support the person paid for with their own wages is theirs, even if you paid the wages, and a person living in their own home provides its fair rental value).

      Count lodging at its fair rental value and property, such as a car the person bought with their own funds, at its fair market value.

      Payments you receive from a child placement agency, state or county for a foster child's support are support provided by the agency, state or county, and benefits a state provides to this person as a needy person (welfare, food benefits, housing, etc.) are support provided by the state, not by the person.

      Do not count a scholarship the person received as a student.

      Social security benefits the person receives and uses for their own support are support the person provided.
  - dependent.joint_return (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Is a joint return being filed for 2025 that includes this person (including one filed by this person with a spouse who died during 2025, or by this person's surviving spouse after this person died during 2025), other than one filed only to claim a refund of withheld or estimated tax?
  - dependent.citizen (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Was this person a U.S. citizen, U.S. national or U.S. resident alien? (Answer yes for your adopted child who lived with you all year as a member of your household, if you are a U.S. citizen or U.S. national.)
  - dependent.canada_mexico (printed '(helper)', type Bool): If not a U.S. citizen, national or resident alien, was this person a resident of Canada or Mexico?
  - dependent.married (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Was this person married at the end of 2025 (a person legally separated under a decree of divorce or separate maintenance is not married), or is a joint return being filed for 2025 that includes this person (including one filed by this person with a spouse who died during 2025, or by this person's surviving spouse after this person died during 2025)?
  - dependent.tin (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Did this person have an SSN, ITIN or ATIN issued on or before the due date of your 2025 return (including extensions), or have you applied for an ITIN or ATIN for this person by then? An ITIN that has expired counts only if it has been renewed, or renewal applied for, by then.
  - dependent.work_ssn (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Did this person have an SSN valid for employment, issued before the due date of your 2025 return (including extensions)? If the card says "Valid for Work Only With DHS Authorization", answer yes only if the DHS authorization is valid.
  - dependent.gross_income (printed '(helper)', type NonNegDollars): This person's gross income for 2025, which is all income in the form of money, property, and services that isn't exempt from tax. Include gross receipts from rental property without deducting taxes, repairs or other expenses, a partner's share of the gross (not net) partnership income, business sales minus the cost of goods sold in a manufacturing, merchandising or mining business, and all taxable unemployment compensation, taxable social security benefits and certain amounts received as scholarship and fellowship grants. Do not include nontaxable social security benefits or other tax-exempt income.
  - dependent.supported (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Did you provide over half of this person's support in 2025?

      Total support is the amount spent for the person's food, lodging, clothing, education, medical and dental care, recreation, transportation and similar necessities, including their tax-exempt income, savings and borrowed amounts used for their support and GI Bill tuition payments and allowances.

      It does not include:
      - income, social security or Medicare taxes the person paid from their own income
      - life insurance premiums
      - funeral expenses
      - a scholarship your child received as a student
      - Survivors' and Dependents' Educational Assistance payments used for the support of the child who receives them

      TANF or other public assistance payments you received and used to support this person count as support you provided.

      Count:
      - lodging at its fair rental value, which includes a reasonable allowance for furniture, appliances, heat and other utilities provided
        - a person living in their own home provides its fair rental value
        - if you provide only part of the lodging and the person supplies the rest, divide the fair rental value by how much each of you provides
        - if you live rent free in this person's home, reduce your support by the fair rental value of the lodging they provide you
      - property at its fair market value
      - expenses for the whole household, such as food, divided among its members

      Medical insurance premiums you pay, including premiums for supplementary Medicare coverage, are support you provide; medical insurance benefits, including Medicare benefits, are not support.

      Count both the government's part and the part taken out of your military pay of an Armed Forces dependency allotment, and a tax-exempt military quarters allowance, as yours, and count payments you make for the person's child or dependent care as yours.

      A lump-sum advance payment to a home for the aged for a relative's care for life, based on their life expectancy, counts each year as the payment divided by that life expectancy.

      A capital item such as furniture, an appliance or a car counts only if bought for the person and given to them, not one the household shares or that you own and let them use.

      The person's own funds are support only if actually spent for their support.

      Payments you receive from a child placement agency, state or county for a foster child's support are support provided by the agency, state or county, not by you; your unreimbursed foster care expenses are not support you provided if you are in the trade or business of providing foster care or if they are deductible as a charitable contribution.

      Support your child paid for with the child's own wages is not yours, even if you paid the wages.

      Benefits a state provides to this person as a needy person (welfare, food benefits, housing, etc.) are support provided by the state, not by you; but payments based on need are not counted entirely as this person's support if part of them can be shown not to have been used for it.

      When spouses receive social security benefits by one check made out to both of them, half is support of each spouse unless they can show otherwise.

      Alimony or separate maintenance payments to a spouse (or similar payments from an estate or trust) are not support of a dependent.

      The year you provide support is the year you pay for it, even with borrowed money you repay later.
  - dependent.kidnapped (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Is this person a child who was kidnapped by someone who isn't a member of your family or the child's family, whom law enforcement authorities presume to have been kidnapped, and who was missing because of the kidnapping at some time during 2025 (a kidnapping in 2025, or an earlier kidnapping before the child's return)? Answer yes even if law enforcement first presumed the kidnapping after 2025, or the child has since been returned.
  - dependent.other_claimant (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Does this person meet the conditions to be the qualifying child of any other person for 2025, under the dependent rules or under the EIC rules (which have no support test)?
  - dependent.other_nontaxpayer (printed '(helper)', type Bool): If this person meets the conditions to be the qualifying child of another person: is every such other person not a taxpayer for 2025? A person is not a taxpayer if they are not required to file a 2025 U.S. income tax return and either do not file one or file only to get a refund of withheld income tax or estimated tax paid (claiming no earned income credit or other tax credit or deduction), or if they are not a U.S. citizen and have no U.S. gross income.
  - dependent.divorced_rule (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Does the special rule for children of divorced or separated parents, or parents who lived apart (including parents who never married), apply to this person, for example because a Form 8332 release or a decree lets the noncustodial parent claim the child? Answer yes whether you are the custodial parent who signed the release (or whose decree or agreement lets the other parent claim the child) or the noncustodial parent using it. A release the custodial parent has revoked still applies for 2025 unless written notice of the revocation was given (or reasonable efforts were made to give it) to the noncustodial parent in 2024 or earlier.
  - dependent.multiple_support (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Did you and others together provide over half of this person's support for 2025, with no one person providing over half (a multiple support situation), whether you or another contributor claims the person?
  - dependent.parent_home_paid (printed '(helper)', type Bool): If this person is your parent, did you pay over half the cost of keeping up a home that was their main home for all of 2025 (or, if your parent died in 2025, for the entire part of 2025 your parent was alive)?

      A rest home or home for the elderly counts as your parent's main home if you paid over half the cost of keeping your parent there.

      Cost of keeping up a home includes expenses such as:
      - rent
      - mortgage interest
      - real estate taxes
      - insurance on the home
      - repairs
      - utilities
      - food eaten in the home

      It does not include:
      - the cost of clothing, education, medical treatment, vacations, life insurance, or transportation
      - the value of your services or those of a member of your household

      Money your parent spent on the home out of their own income, such as Social Security, counts as paid by your parent, not by you.

      TANF or other similar payments you received and used to keep up the home count as paid by you.
  - dependent.born_died (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Was this person born in 2025, or did this person die in 2025? A child born in 2025 is born alive only if state or local law treats the child as born alive and an official document, such as a birth certificate, shows the live birth (even if the child lived only for a moment). Do not list a stillborn child.
  - dependent.placed (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Did you adopt this person in 2025, or was this person lawfully placed with you for legal adoption by you, or placed with you as an eligible foster child, during 2025?
  - dependent.part_year_home (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Was your home this person's home for more than half of the part of 2025 they were alive (or, for a person adopted or placed with you in 2025, more than half of the time since the adoption or placement), counting temporary absences for school, vacation, business, medical care, military service or detention in a juvenile facility as time lived with you?
  - dependent.younger (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Was this person younger than you (or, if you file jointly, younger than you or your spouse; the person need not be younger than both of you)?
  - dependent.us_half (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Did this person live with you in the United States (the 50 states and the District of Columbia) for more than half of 2025?

      If you were on extended active military duty outside the United States, your main home is considered to be in the United States during that duty.

      Temporary absences for school, vacation, business, medical care, military service or juvenile detention count as time lived with you; time a child presumed kidnapped was missing because of the kidnapping does not.

      A child born or who died in 2025 counts if your home was the child's home for more than half the time the child was alive.

      A child you adopted, who was lawfully placed with you for legal adoption, or who was placed with you as an eligible foster child in 2025 counts if your main home was the child's main home for more than half the time since the adoption or placement.
  - dependent.eic_ssn (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Did this person have a social security number issued on or before the due date of your 2025 return (including extensions) that is valid for the EIC? Any number issued by the Social Security Administration is valid unless the card says "Not Valid for Employment" and the number was issued solely to apply for or receive a federally funded benefit; a card that says "Valid for Work Only With DHS Authorization" is valid only while the authorization is valid.
  - dependent.spouse_relative (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Is this person related to your spouse in one of these ways?

      - your spouse's child, stepchild or foster child, or a descendant of any of them
      - your spouse's brother, sister, half brother, half sister, stepbrother or stepsister
      - your spouse's father, mother, grandparent or other direct ancestor (not a foster parent)
      - your spouse's stepfather or stepmother
      - a son or daughter of your spouse's brother, sister, half brother or half sister
      - a brother or sister of your spouse's father or mother
      - your spouse's son-in-law, daughter-in-law, father-in-law, mother-in-law, brother-in-law or sister-in-law

      A relationship established by marriage is not ended by death or divorce.

      (Asked only on a joint return, for a person who is not one of these relatives of yours.)
  - dependent.qr_all_year (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Did this person live with you all of 2025 as a member of your household?

      Count as time lived with you:
      - a temporary absence by you or the person for special circumstances, such as illness, education, business, vacation, military service or detention in a juvenile facility
      - a stay in a nursing home for an indefinite period to receive constant medical care

      Answer Yes also for:
      - a person who died in 2025 and lived with you as a member of your household until death
      - a child born in 2025 who lived with you as a member of your household for the rest of the year, apart from any required hospital stay following birth
      - a person you adopted, who was lawfully placed with you for legal adoption, or who was placed with you as an eligible foster child in 2025, if your main home was the person's main home for the entire time since the adoption or placement
  - dependent.lawful (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Did your relationship with this person not violate local law? (Asked for a person who is a Step 4 relative only as a member of your household all year, such as a sibling's grandchild or a step sibling's child, or who is no relative.)
  - dependent.absence_no_return (printed '(helper)', type Bool): In counting the time this person lived with you in 2025, did you count as time lived with you an absence by you or the person from which it was not reasonable to assume the absent person would return to the home, or during which you did not continue to keep up the home? (Asked when this person may qualify you for head of household.)
  - dependent.dc_under13 (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Was this child under age 13 for part of 2025, when you paid for their care? (A child who turned 13 in 2025 is a qualifying person only for the part of the year before their 13th birthday.)
  - dependent.dc_incapable (printed '(helper)', type Bool): In 2025, was this person physically or mentally not able to care for themselves (unable to dress, clean or feed themselves because of a physical or mental disability, or needing constant attention to prevent them from injuring themselves or others)? This is a different test from permanent and total disability.
  - dependent.dc_paid (printed '(helper)', type NonNegDollars): Qualified expenses you incurred and paid in 2025 for this person's care while they were a qualifying person (under age 13, or not able to care for themselves), so that you (and your spouse, if filing jointly) could work or look for work (unpaid volunteer work, or work for a nominal salary, is not work)

      Include:
      - care you prepaid in 2024 for 2025
      - your share of employment taxes on a care provider's wages
      - expenses paid with dependent care benefits (reimbursed from a dependent care account or paid by your employer to a provider you chose); Form 2441 subtracts the benefits you exclude

      Include also:
      - household services needed to run your home if they were partly for this person's care (for example a cook, maid, babysitter, housekeeper or cleaning person, but not a chauffeur, bartender or gardener)
      - meals your housekeeper eats in your home because of the job and the extra cost of lodging the housekeeper in your home
      - a care provider's transportation of this person to or from the place of care (but not other transportation, or the cost of bringing the provider to your home)
      - agency fees, deposits, application fees and other indirect costs you had to pay to get care (but not a forfeited deposit for care that was not provided)

      Do not include the value of care your employer furnished itself (at your workplace or through a care provider your employer hired).

      Do not include:
      - 2024 care paid in 2025
      - 2025 care paid in 2026
      - payments for care in 2026
      - amounts reimbursed by a state social services agency
      - payments to a provider who may not be listed (your spouse, your dependent, your child under 19, or the parent of your child under 13)
      - expenses you claim as medical expenses
      - the cost of food, lodging, education, clothing or entertainment other than the housekeeper's meals and lodging above (food or schooling incidental to a child's care that can't be separated from the total cost may be included, but never schooling for a child in kindergarten or above)
      - expenses for an overnight camp, a summer school or a tutoring program
      - care outside your home for a qualifying person other than your dependent under 13 who does not regularly spend at least 8 hours a day in your home
      - care at a dependent care center that does not meet all applicable state and local regulations
      - child support payments

      If you (or your spouse) worked or looked for work during only part of the period the expenses cover, count the expenses only for the days of work or looking for work (a day you work at least 1 hour is a day of work).

      But include:
      - care you had to pay for anyway during a short, temporary absence from work, such as a vacation or minor illness (2 weeks or less, or longer depending on the circumstances)
      - for part-time work, care you must pay for weekly, monthly or in another way that covers both days worked and days not worked

      If part of an expense is work related and part is for other purposes, count only the work-related part, unless only a small part is for other purposes.

      Zero if none
  - dependent.dc_over12_care (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Of the care expenses included for this person on Form 2441, line 2, was any care provided when the person was age 13 or older and physically or mentally unable to care for themselves? Answer only for the care included on this form, not their age at the end of the year.
  - dependent.ptc_files (printed '(gate)', type Bool): Is this dependent required to file a 2025 income tax return because of their income, by Chart B in the Form 1040 instructions (unearned income over $1,350, earned income over $15,750, or gross income over the larger of $1,350 and earned income up to $15,300 plus $450, for a single dependent under 65 and not blind)? Answer no if the dependent files only to claim a refund of withheld or estimated tax.
  - dependent.ptc_magi (printed '(helper)', type Dollars): This dependent's modified AGI for 2025: the AGI on their return (line 11a), plus their tax-exempt interest (line 2a), plus any Form 2555 amounts, plus the part of their social security benefits that is not taxable (line 6a less line 6b); negative if it was a loss
  - dependent.edu (printed '(gate)', type Bool): Did anyone pay qualified education expenses in 2025 for this dependent at an eligible educational institution (a college, university, vocational school, or other postsecondary school eligible for federal student aid)?
  - dependent.edu_1098t (printed '(gate)', type Bool): Did the student receive a 2025 Form 1098-T from the educational institution? Answer yes also if the institution was not required to furnish one (for example a qualified nonresident alien, expenses paid entirely with scholarships or under a formal billing arrangement, or only courses for which no academic credit is awarded), or if the institution did not furnish it and, after February 2, 2026, but before you file, you or the student requested it, you fully cooperated with the institution's efforts to gather the information needed to furnish it, and you can show the enrollment and the payments.
  - dependent.edu_inst (printed '(helper)', type NonNegDollars): Qualified education expenses paid in 2025 for this student, by anyone, that had to be paid to the institution, for academic periods beginning in 2025 or in the first 3 months of 2026:

      - tuition
      - required enrollment fees
      - books, supplies and equipment required to be paid to the institution as a condition of enrollment or attendance

      Use the amounts actually paid, which may differ from Form 1098-T box 1.

      Do not include:
      - amounts paid in 2024 or 2026
      - room and board
      - insurance
      - medical expenses (including student health fees)
      - transportation
      - expenses deducted elsewhere (for example on Schedule C)
      - expenses used for a tax-free distribution from a Coverdell ESA or qualified tuition program (529 plan)
      - a course or other education involving sports, games or hobbies, or any noncredit course, unless it is part of the student's degree program or, where this student can't get the American opportunity credit, it helps the student acquire or improve job skills

      Zero if none
  - dependent.edu_mat (printed '(helper)', type NonNegDollars): Other course materials paid for in 2025 for this student: books, supplies and equipment needed for a course of study that did not have to be paid to the institution (they count for the American opportunity credit only); the same exclusions as for the expenses paid to the institution; zero if none
  - dependent.edu_a2025 (printed '(helper)', type NonNegDollars): Tax-free educational assistance received in 2025 for this student's 2025 expenses: the tax-free part of scholarships and fellowship grants (including Pell grants; Form 1098-T box 5 may help), tax-free employer-provided educational assistance, veterans' educational assistance, and other tax-free educational assistance. Do not include wages, loans, gifts, inheritances, withdrawals from personal savings, emergency financial aid grants, or a scholarship the student includes in income (for you or your spouse, the amount on Schedule 1 line 8r); zero if none
  - dependent.edu_a2026 (printed '(helper)', type NonNegDollars): Tax-free educational assistance received in 2026, before you file this return, that is allocable to an academic period of these 2025 expenses; zero if none
  - dependent.edu_refunds (printed '(helper)', type NonNegDollars): Refunds of these 2025 qualified education expenses received in 2025, or in 2026 before you file this return; zero if none
  - dependent.edu_over (printed '(gate)', type Bool): Was there an academic period for which the tax-free educational assistance and refunds allocable to it were more than that period's qualified education expenses that had to be paid to the institution (tuition, required fees and course materials required to be paid to the institution)?
  - dependent.edu_line23 (printed '23', type Bool): Has the American opportunity credit been claimed (by you or anyone else) for this student for any 4 tax years before 2025?
  - dependent.edu_line24 (printed '24', type Bool): Was the student enrolled at least half-time (Form 1098-T box 8) for at least one academic period that began in 2025, or in the first 3 months of 2026 if paid for in 2025, at an eligible educational institution, in a program leading toward a postsecondary degree, certificate or other recognized postsecondary educational credential?
  - dependent.edu_line25 (printed '25', type Bool): Did the student complete the first 4 years of postsecondary education before 2025, as determined by the institution (4 years of academic credit, not counting credit awarded solely for proficiency examinations)?
  - dependent.edu_line26 (printed '26', type Bool): Was the student convicted, before the end of 2025, of a federal or state felony for possession or distribution of a controlled substance?
  - dependent.edu_ein (printed '(gate)', type Bool): Do you have the educational institution's employer identification number (EIN), from Form 1098-T or from the institution?
  - dependent.edu_tin_issued (printed '(helper)', type Bool): Was this student's SSN, ITIN or ATIN issued on or before the due date of your 2025 return (including extensions)? Answer no if an ITIN or ATIN had only been applied for by then, even if it has been or will be issued later.

This is a CLOSED input interface: do not introduce any new `op: input` line, even if the full IRS
form has additional boxes. Sources excluded by the screening questions are outside this slice;
their form lines must be represented within that declared scope, not by inventing extra inputs.
If the supplied text/interface does not determine a requested result, record that limitation in
a note rather than adding an unprovided input. Derive computed questions from the named interfaces.

Only names in that year-parameter list may be used with `op: param` or a parameter operand.
An id listed below as a line or result interface is not a parameter; copy that line by its string id.

A parameter whose value is a dictionary keyed by filing status automatically selects the current
return's filing status and returns the amount of the declared type. Read it with `op: param` (or
as a parameter operand); do not invent a table named after the parameter. For a constant-valued
line use a declared parameter of the matching type (for example a zero-valued money parameter).
`expr: {const: 0}` is an operand, not a line expression; every line expression needs an `op`.

Use only these table ids. Infer which table applies from the IRS pages; the catalog does not
tell you which result should use a lookup. If no supplied table supports an instruction, note
that limitation instead of inventing a table or a new operation.

If the IRS says to STOP because no tax or credit applies, carry that condition to the requested
tax/credit/deduction result as zero; do not continue the arithmetic past that STOP or leave its
effect only in a note. If the instruction instead says to STOP and use an earlier amount, return
that amount. Derive the stopping condition from the archived text; none is supplied by the project.

Keyed collections (Form W-2s, payers, sales, gifts): a total over entries is written
{op: sum_over, collection: <id>, field: <entry field id>, where: <test over entry fields>}; a nested
collection is summed within each parent entry, and the parent's per-entry totals summed again:

Property you inherited is long-term however long you held it, unless the decedent died in 2010 and the executor elected to file Form 8939 (Revenue Procedure 2011-41 then sets the holding period). To count the holding period, begin on the day after you acquired the property and include the day you sold it. If your basis in property you received as a gift is the donor's adjusted basis, your holding period began on the day the donor's did. A block acquired on different dates is entered as separate transactions for its short-term and long-term parts. Stock or securities bought in a wash sale include the holding period of those sold. New stock from a nontaxable stock dividend or a spin-off, and nontaxable stock rights, include the holding period of the old stock; stock from a taxable stock dividend begins on the date of distribution, stock acquired by exercising stock rights on the date the right was exercised, and property acquired by exercising an option the day after the option was exercised. A nonbusiness bad debt is short-term, and so is a gain or loss under the net asset value (NAV) method for a money market fund. A short sale is reported in the year it closes. A gain on closing a short sale is short-term if, on the date of the short sale, you held substantially identical property for 1 year or less, or if you acquired substantially identical property after the short sale but on or before the date you closed it; a loss on a short sale is long-term if, on the date of the short sale, you held substantially identical property for more than 1 year. For securities traded on an established market, the holding period begins the day after the trade date you bought them and ends on the trade date you sold them, not the settlement date. U.S. Treasury notes and bonds bought at auction start the day after the Treasury gives notice of acceptance to successful bidders, and those bought by subscription at a specified yield the day after the subscription is submitted. For shares bought for you by an automatic investment service, the holding period starts the day after the bank's purchase date; full shares are bought first and fractional shares last, and a share bought over more than one purchase date has a split holding period. Investment property acquired in a nontaxable trade, with a basis figured in whole or in part from the property traded, is held from the day after you acquired the old property. If your basis in a gift is its fair market value, the holding period starts the day after the gift. Property you elected to treat as sold and reacquired on January 1, 2001 (January 2, 2001, for readily tradable stock) is treated as acquired on that date. Real property bought under an unconditional contract is held from the day after you received title or took possession and assumed the burdens and privileges of ownership, whichever came first; for real property you repossessed after selling it, include the time you held it before the original sale and after the repossession, but not the time between. The premium you receive for writing an option that is not exercised (or repurchased) is a short-term capital gain unless you are in the business of writing options; an option you bought and did not exercise is treated as sold on the date it expired. Any capital gain or loss on the sale, exchange or termination of a contract to sell property is short-term however long you held the contract, and a loss on a securities futures contract to sell is treated as a loss on closing a short sale. Stock received in a demutualization that qualifies as a tax-free reorganization includes the period you held the equity interest in the mutual company.; sales.route (form sales; printed '(helper)'; type Enum(sales_route)): input Report this transaction in detail on Form 8949 or directly in Schedule D aggregate totals; Property you received in a taxable trade has a basis equal to its fair market value at the time of the trade; property from a nontaxable or partly nontaxable trade takes the adjusted basis of the property you gave up, increased by any cash you paid, additional costs and gain recognized, and reduced by any cash or unlike property you received, loss recognized and liability of yours assumed. A wash sale occurs when you sell or trade stock or securities (including a contract or option to acquire or sell stock or securities) at a loss and, within 30 days before or after the sale, you buy substantially identical stock or securities, acquire them in a fully taxable trade, acquire a contract or option to buy them, or acquire substantially identical stock or securities for your IRA or Roth IRA; it is also a wash sale if your spouse or a corporation you control buys substantially identical stock. helper of this document that you reference in your own reply, even if a familiar name occurs
elsewhere in the catalog. Fields of another form or collection that the catalog lists are
referenced by their ids (a collection's fields through sum_over); do not redefine them. Only the
listed input lines are already provided locally. A foreign collection field cannot be
used as a return-level refusal gate without an explicitly defined, correctly bound aggregate.

invent default answers. No authored formulas or expected results are supplied.

# Output

Keep the exact declared type on every requested result id. A mathematically nonnegative result
may still have a published Dollars interface; do not replace it with NonNegDollars. Before
returning, check that each local computed reference is defined in this reply and each foreign
reference is in the supplied catalog. These are syntax/interface requirements, not tax formulas.

Return ONLY a YAML document of the form

lines:
  - id: <exact id from the required-line lists above>
    printed: "<printed>"
    label: "<the IRS wording>"
    type: <Dollars | NonNegDollars | Rate | Count | Bool | Enum(name)>
    expr: <one atom>
    refuses: [<screening input id>, ...]   # optional
    shown_when: <Bool line id of this document>   # required where the IRS text makes the line's display conditional
  - ...

plus any helper lines you need (printed: "(helper)", ids "dependent.h_<name>"). No prose before
or after the YAML. Where the IRS text leaves a point genuinely open, choose a reading and add a
`note:` field on that line saying what was open and what you chose.

`shown_when` is required on every printed line whose display the IRS text makes conditional
(the form or worksheet shows the line, or an amount on it, for some returns or entries only): give the
Bool line of this document that is true exactly when the line is shown. Leave it out only on a line
the IRS text always shows. The data-model table above calls the field optional because most lines are
always shown, not because it may be omitted where the text conditions a line.

===== i1040gi (2025; printed date 2026-02-25) page 10 of 126, as laid out =====
Chart B—For Children and Other Dependents (See Who Qualifies as Your Dependent, later.)
If your parent (or someone else) can claim you as a dependent, use this chart to see if you must file a return.
In this chart, unearned income includes taxable interest, ordinary dividends, and capital gain distributions. It also includes
unemployment compensation, taxable social security benefits, pensions, annuities, and distributions of unearned income from a trust.
Earned income includes salaries, wages, tips, professional fees, and taxable scholarship and fellowship grants. Gross income is the
total of your unearned and earned income.

Single dependents. Were you either age 65 or older or blind?
       No. You must file a return if any of the following apply.
          • Your unearned income was over $1,350.
          • Your earned income was over $15,750.
          • Your gross income was more than the larger of—
             • $1,350, or
             • Your earned income (up to $15,300) plus $450.
       Yes. You must file a return if any of the following apply.
          • Your unearned income was over $3,350 ($5,350 if 65 or older and blind).
          • Your earned income was over $17,750 ($19,750 if 65 or older and blind).
          • Your gross income was more than the larger of—
             • $3,350 ($5,350 if 65 or older and blind), or
             • Your earned income (up to $15,300) plus $2,450 ($4,450 if 65 or older and blind).
Married dependents. Were you either age 65 or older or blind?
       No. You must file a return if any of the following apply.
          • Your unearned income was over $1,350.
          • Your earned income was over $15,750.
          • Your gross income was at least $5 and your spouse files a separate return and itemizes deductions.
          • Your gross income was more than the larger of—
             • $1,350, or
             • Your earned income (up to $15,300) plus $450.
       Yes. You must file a return if any of the following apply.
          • Your unearned income was over $2,950 ($4,550 if 65 or older and blind).
          • Your earned income was over $17,350 ($18,950 if 65 or older and blind).
          • Your gross income was at least $5 and your spouse files a separate return and itemizes deductions.
          • Your gross income was more than the larger of—
             • $2,950 ($4,550 if 65 or older and blind), or
             • Your earned income (up to $15,300) plus $2,050 ($3,650 if 65 or older and blind).

U.S. residents for the entire year if both of you are U.S. citizens or residents at the end of the year and either (or both) of you were a nonresident at the beginning of the year (the dual-status spouse(s)). You can only make this choice for 1 year, and it does not apply to any future years. If you and your spouse are making either of these choices to be treated as U.S. residents for 2025, check the box in the Filing Status section and enter the name of the nonresident spouse or dual-status spouse(s) (whichever applies to you) in the entry space. Also check the box and enter their name if you and your nonresident spouse made the choice to be treated as residents in a prior year and the choice remains in effect.
To make either choice for 2025, you and your spouse must file a joint return and attach a statement, signed by both spouses, to your return. To find out what information must be included in the statement, as well as more information on these choices, see Nonresident Spouse Treated as a Resident for nonresident aliens and Choosing Resident Alien Status for dual-status aliens in Pub. 519.

CAUTION
Married Filing Separately
Check the “Married filing separately” box in the Filing Status section on page 1 of Form 1040 or 1040-SR if you are married at the end of 2025 and file a separate return. Enter your spouse’s name in the entry space. Be sure to enter your spouse’s SSN or ITIN in the space for spouse’s SSN on Form 1040 or 1040-SR. If your spouse doesn’t have and isn’t required to have an SSN or ITIN, enter “NRA” in the entry space.
For electronic filing, enter the spouse’s name or “NRA” if the spouse doesn’t have an SSN or ITIN in the entry space.
If you are married and file a separate return, you generally report only your own income, deductions, and credits. Generally, you are responsible only for the tax on your own income. Different rules apply to people in community property states; see Pub. 555.
However, you will usually pay more tax than if you use another filing status for which you qualify. Also, if you file a
separate return, you can’t take the deduction for qualified tips, the deduction for qualified overtime, the enhanced senior deduction, the student loan interest deduction, or the education credits, and you will only be able to take the earned income credit and child and dependent care credit in very limited circumstances. You also can’t take the standard deduction if your spouse itemizes deductions. For situations when you might want to file separately, see Joint and several tax liability, earlier.
You may be able to file as head
of household if you had a child

living with you and you lived apart from your spouse during the last 6 months of 2025. See Married persons who live apart, later.
Head of Household
You can check the “Head of household” box in the Filing Status section on page 1 of Form 1040 or 1040-SR if you are unmarried and provide a home for certain other persons. You are considered unmarried for this purpose if any of the following applies. • You were legally separated according to your state law under a decree of divorce or separate maintenance at the end of 2025. But if, at the end of 2025, your divorce wasn’t final (an interlocutory decree), you are considered married. • You are married but lived apart from your spouse for the last 6 months of 2025 and you meet the other rules under Married persons who live apart, later. • You are married and your spouse was a nonresident alien at any time during the year and the election to treat the alien spouse as a resident alien is not made. See Nonresident aliens and dual-status aliens, earlier. Check the “Head of household” box only if you are unmarried (or considered unmarried) and either Test 1 or Test 2 applies. Test 1. You paid over half the cost of keeping up a home that was the main home for all of 2025 of your parent whom you can claim as a dependent, except under a multiple support agreement (see Who Qualifies as Your Dependent, later). Your parent didn’t have to live with you.
Test 2. You paid over half the cost of keeping up a home in which you lived and in which one of the following also lived for more than half of the year (if half or less, see Exception to time lived with you, later). 1. Any person whom you can claim as a dependent. But don’t include: a. Your child whom you claim as your dependent because of the rule for Children of divorced or separated parents under Who Qualifies as Your Dependent, later; b. Any person who is your dependent only because the person lived with you for all of 2025; or c. Any person you claimed as a dependent under a multiple support agreement. See Who Qualifies as Your Dependent, later. 2. Your unmarried qualifying child who isn’t your dependent. 3. Your married qualifying child who isn’t your dependent only because you can be claimed as a dependent on someone else’s 2025 return. 4. Your qualifying child who, even though you are the custodial parent, isn’t your dependent because of the rule for Children of divorced or separated parents under Who Qualifies as Your Dependent, later. If the child isn’t claimed as your dependent, enter the child’s name in the entry space below qualifying surviving spouse. If you don’t enter the name, it will take us longer to process your return.

Married persons who live apart. Even if you weren’t divorced or legally separated at the end of 2025, you are considered unmarried if all of the following apply. • You lived apart from your spouse for the last 6 months of 2025. Temporary absences for special circumstances, such as for business, medical care, school, or military service, count as time lived in the home. • You file a separate return from your spouse. • You paid over half the cost of keeping up your home for 2025. • Your home was the main home of your child, stepchild, or foster child for more than half of 2025 (if half or less, see Exception to time lived with you, earlier). • You can claim this child as your dependent or could claim the child except that the child’s other parent can claim the child under the rule for Children of divorced or separated parents under Who Qualifies as Your Dependent, later.
Adopted child. An adopted child is always treated as your own child. An adopted child includes a child lawfully placed with you for legal adoption.
Foster child. A foster child is any child placed with you by an authorized placement agency or by judgment, decree, or other order of any court of competent jurisdiction.
Qualifying Surviving Spouse
You can check the “Qualifying surviving spouse” box in the Filing Status section on page 1 of Form 1040 or 1040-SR and use joint return tax rates for 2025 if all of the following apply. 1. Your spouse died in 2023 or 2024 and you didn’t remarry before the end of 2025. 2. You have a child or stepchild (not a foster child) whom you can claim as a dependent or could claim as a dependent except that, for 2025: a. The child had gross income of $5,200 or more, b. The child filed a joint return, or c. You could be claimed as a dependent on someone else’s return.
If the child isn’t claimed as your dependent, enter the child’s name in the entry space. If you don’t enter the name, it will take us longer to process your return. 3. This child lived in your home for all of 2025. If the child didn’t live with you for the required time, see Exception to time lived with you, later. 4. You paid over half the cost of keeping up your home. 5. You could have filed a joint return with your spouse the year your spouse died, even if you didn’t actually do so.
If your spouse died in 2025, you can't file as qualifying surviving spouse. Instead, see the instructions for Married Filing Jointly, earlier. Adopted child. Digital Assets
Digital assets are any digital representations of value that are recorded on a cryptographically secured distributed ledger or any similar technology. For example, digital assets include non-fungible tokens (NFTs) and virtual currencies, such as cryptocurrencies and stablecoins. If a particular asset has the characteristics of a digital asset, it will be treated as a digital asset for federal income tax purposes.
Check the “Yes” box next to the question on digital assets on page 1 of Form 1040 or 1040-SR if at any time during 2025, you (a) received (as a reward, award, or payment for property or services); or (b) sold, exchanged, or otherwise disposed of a digital asset (or any financial interest in any digital asset).
For example, check “Yes” if at any time during 2025, you: • Received digital assets as payment for property or services provided; • Received digital assets as a result of a reward or award;

===== i1040gi (2025; printed date 2026-02-25) page 17 of 126, in reading order =====
Who Qualifies as Your Dependent
Dependents, Qualifying Child for Child Tax Credit, and Credit for Other Dependents
Follow the steps in the following flowchart to find out if a person qualifies as your dependent and to find out if your dependent qualifies you to take the child tax credit or the credit for other dependents. If you have more than four dependents, check the box under Dependents on page 1 of Form 1040 or 1040-SR and include a statement showing the information requested in the Dependents section.
The dependents you claim are those you list by name and SSN in the Dependents section on Form 1040 or 1040-SR.
TIP
Before you begin. See the definition of Social security number, later. If you want to claim the child tax credit, you must have a valid SSN, which means it must be valid for employment and issued before the due date of your return (including extensions). If you are filing a joint return, only one spouse is required to have a valid SSN to be eligible for the CTC and ACTC. The other spouse must have either an SSN or ITIN, and it must have been issued on or before the due date of the return (including extensions). If you want to claim the credit for other dependents, you, and your spouse if filing jointly, must have either an SSN or ITIN issued on or before the due date of your 2025 return (including extensions).
Do You Have a Qualifying Child?
Step 1
A qualifying child is your...
Son, daughter, stepchild, foster child, brother, sister, stepbrother, stepsister, half brother, half sister, or a descendant of any of them (for example, your grandchild, niece, or nephew)
AND
was ...
Under age 19 at the end of 2025 and younger than you (or your spouse if filing jointly)
or
Under age 24 at the end of 2025, a full-time student (defined later), and younger than you (or your spouse if filing jointly). If the child is a full-time student, check the “Full-time student” box on row (6) of the Dependents section on page 1 of Form 1040 or 1040-SR
or Any age and permanently and totally disabled (defined later). If the child is permanently and totally disabled, check the “Permanently and totally disabled” box on row (6) of the Dependents section on page 1 of Form 1040 or 1040-SR.
AND
Who didn't provide over half of their own support for 2025 (see Pub. 501)
AND
Who isn't filing a joint return for 2025 or is filing a joint return for 2025 only to claim a refund of withheld income tax or estimated tax paid (see Pub. 501 for details and examples)
AND
Who lived with you for more than half of 2025. If the child didn’t live with you for the required time, see Exception to time lived with you, later. If the child lived with you for more than half of 2025, check the “Yes” box (box (a)) on row (5) of the Dependents section on page 1 of Form 1040 or 1040-SR.
If the child meets the conditions to be a qualifying child of any other person (other than your spouse if filing jointly) for 2025, see Qualifying child of more than one person, later.

Is Your Qualifying Relative Your Dependent?
Step 4
A qualifying relative is your...
Son, daughter, stepchild, foster child, or a descendant of any of them (for example, your grandchild)
or
Brother, sister, half brother, half sister, or a son or daughter of any of them (for example, your niece or nephew)
or
Father, mother, or an ancestor or sibling of either of them (for example, your grandmother, grandfather, aunt, or uncle)
or
Stepbrother, stepsister, stepfather, stepmother, son-in-law, daughter-in-law, father-in-law, mother-in-law, brother-in-law, or sister-in-law
or
Any other person (other than your spouse) who lived with you all year as a member of your household if your relationship didn’t violate local law. If the person didn’t live with you for the required time, see Exception to time lived with you, later.
AND
Who wasn’t a qualifying child (see Step 1) of any taxpayer for 2025. For this purpose, a person isn’t a taxpayer if the person isn’t required to file a U.S. income tax return and either doesn’t file such a return or files only to get a refund of withheld income tax or estimated tax paid. See Pub. 501 for details and examples.

For whom you provided over half of the person’s support in 2025. But see Children of divorced or separated parents, Multiple support agreements, and Kidnapped child, later.
